This explains the constraints on the integration radii
r,r^{\prime}
in
Theorem
2.5
.

Remark 3.12
.

Using the canonical anticommutation relations, it is possible to see
that there is a single meromorphic function
\kappa_{1,2}(z,w)
with
a pole at
z=w
, and that its two expressions in (
3.62
)
correspond to Laurent expansions in different annuli. Furthermore,
the fermionic reflection relations (
3.42
) imply that

\kappa_{1,2}(z,w)=t\frac{v+w}{v-w}\kappa_{1,1}\left(z,\frac{v^{2}}{w}\right)=t^{-1}\frac{z-v}{z+v}\kappa_{2,2}\left(\frac{v^{2}}{z},w\right).

(3.63)

Evaluation of fermionic propagators.

We now turn to the
evaluation of the
\kappa
’s. In the case of one free boundary
(
u=0
), the computations are rather easy, for instance to compute

\kappa_{1,2}(z,w)=\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v,t}\rangle
for
|z|>|w|
we
use (
3.36
) and notice that the only
|S\rangle
that
contribute have charge
0
and correspond to “hook”
partitions. Taking into account signs and the special contribution from

|0\rangle
, we get

\kappa_{1,2}(z,w)=\sum_{k\in\in\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{<0}}(-1)^{k+1/2}\frac{z^{k}}{v^{k}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{>0}}\frac{w^{\ell}}{v^{\ell}}+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}_{<0}}\frac{z^{k}}{w^{k}}=\frac{(zw-v^{2})\sqrt{zw}}{(z+v)(w-v)(z-w)}.

(3.64)

For
v=1
, we obtain the expression announced in (
2.10
),
and we leave the reader derive similarly the expressions for the other
propagators, given in Appendix
C
, to
conclude the proof of Theorem
2.2
.

We may adapt this approach to the case of two free boundaries, but the
computations become involved. A simpler approach is to use
Proposition
3.2
to rewrite the fermions in terms
of vertex operators. For instance, to evaluate
\kappa_{1,2}(z,w)
for

|z|>|w|
, we write

\begin{split}\langle\underline{u,t}|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v,t}\rangle&=\langle\underline{u,t}|(z/w)^{C-1/2}\Gamma_{-}(z)\Gamma^{\prime}_{+}\left(-z^{-1}\right)\Gamma^{\prime}_{-}(-w)\Gamma_{+}\left(w^{-1}\right)|\underline{v,t}\rangle\\
&=\sqrt{\frac{w}{z}}\left(\sum_{c\in 2\mathbb{Z}}(tz/w)^{c}(uv)^{c^{2}/2}\right)\langle\underline{u}|\Gamma_{-}(z)\Gamma^{\prime}_{+}\left(-z^{-1}\right)\Gamma^{\prime}_{-}(-w)\Gamma_{+}\left(w^{-1}\right)|\underline{v}\rangle\end{split}

(3.65)

where we use (
3.39
), (
3.40
) and the commutation
of vertex operators with
R
and
C
. The last factor on the second
line can be identified with the partition function of a certain free
boundary Schur process of length
2
, compare with
Proposition
3.4
. Using Proposition
2.1
to
evaluate this partition function, and recognizing several Pochhammer
symbols and theta functions, we arrive at

\langle\underline{u,t}|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v,t}\rangle=\sqrt{\frac{w}{z}}\theta_{3}\left((tz/w)^{2};(uv)^{4}\right)\frac{((uv)^{2};(uv)^{2})_{\infty}^{2}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(u^{2}zw)\langle\underline{u}|\underline{v}\rangle}{(uv,uz,-uw,-vz^{-1},vw^{-1};uv)_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(w/z)}.

(3.66)

Upon dividing by the normalization (
3.41
), we obtain the
expression of
\kappa_{1,2}(z,w)
announced in (
2.14
). The
expressions for the other propagators, given in
Appendix
C
, can be checked using
(
3.63
) and simple manipulations of Pochhammer symbols and
theta functions. This concludes the proof of
Theorem
2.5
.

3.3.2
Variations on free boundary states

In this section, we explain how to handle the extra weighting

\alpha^{oc}
mentioned in Remark
2.4
. Recall that

|\underline{v}\rangle=\sum_{\lambda}v^{|\lambda|}|\lambda\rangle
. We
define the following companion boundary vectors:

\displaystyle|\underline{v^{ec}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda:\ \lambda\text{ has even columns}}v^{|\lambda|}|\lambda\rangle,

\displaystyle|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda}\alpha^{\#\text{ of odd columns of }\lambda}v^{|\lambda|}|\lambda\rangle,

(3.67)

\displaystyle|\underline{v^{er}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda:\ \lambda\text{ has even rows}}v^{|\lambda|}|\lambda\rangle,

\displaystyle|\underline{v^{\beta,\ or}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\lambda}\beta^{\#\text{ of odd rows of }\lambda}v^{|\lambda|}|\lambda\rangle

where
ec,er,oc,or
stand for respectively
even columns, even
rows, odd columns, odd rows
, and where
\alpha,\beta
are
parameters. Note that

|\underline{v^{1,\ oc}}\rangle=|\underline{v^{1,\ or}}\rangle=|\underline{v}\rangle
while

|\underline{v^{0,\ oc}}\rangle=|\underline{v^{ec}}\rangle
and

|\underline{v^{0,\ or}}\rangle=|\underline{v^{er}}\rangle
. Analogously we may define covectors

\langle\underline{u^{ec}}|
, etc.

Proposition 3.13
.

We have

|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc}}\rangle=\Gamma_{-}(\alpha v)|\underline{v^{ec}}\rangle,\qquad|\underline{v^{\beta,\ or}}\rangle=\Gamma^{\prime}_{-}(\beta v)|\underline{v^{er}}\rangle.

(3.68)

Proof.

The first (resp. second) identity results from the fact that any
partition can be decomposed uniquely into a partition with even
columns (resp. rows), and a horizontal (resp. vertical)
strip.
∎

Noting that
\Gamma_{-}(x)\Gamma^{\prime}_{-}(-x)=1
for any single variable
x
, we
deduce that the modified free boundary states can be expressed in
terms of the original one as

|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc}}\rangle=\Gamma_{-}(\alpha v)\Gamma^{\prime}_{-}(-v)|\underline{v}\rangle,\qquad|\underline{v^{\beta,\ or}}\rangle=\Gamma^{\prime}_{-}(\beta v)\Gamma_{-}(-v)|\underline{v}\rangle.

(3.69)

Replacing
|\underline{v}\rangle
by
|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc}}\rangle
in
Proposition
3.4
, we obtain the partition function and
correlation functions for the free boundary Schur process with the
extra weight
\alpha^{oc}
counting the number of odd columns of

\mu^{(N)}
. By (
3.69
), we readily
see that such modification amounts to replacing the boundary
specialization
\rho_{N}^{-}
by the specialization
\rho_{N}^{-}[\alpha,v]

such that

H(\rho_{N}^{-}[\alpha,v];t)=\frac{1-tv}{1-\alpha tv}H(\rho_{N}^{-};t).

(3.70)

In other words, we are “adding” a specialization in the single
variable
\alpha v
, and “subtracting” (in the sense of plethystic
negation) a variable
v
. Note that
\rho_{N}^{-}[\alpha,v]
is a
priori not nonnegative. Still, this allows to deduce easily the
partition function and correlations functions of the modified process
from Proposition
2.1
and
Theorem
2.5
.
In (
2.14
), changing

\rho_{N}^{-}
into
\rho_{N}^{-}[\alpha,v]
produces some extra factors in

F(i,z)
. These factors will appear in the contour integral
representation (
2.9
), once for
F(i,z)
and once for

F(i^{\prime},w)
. We may conventionally choose to absorb them in a
redefinition of the
\kappa
’s, and keep
F
unchanged: this yields
the announced expression (
2.11
) for
u=0
and
v=1
. It is
not difficult to see that such a redefinition of the
\kappa
’s amounts
to replacing in (
3.62
) the extended free boundary state
|\underline{v,t}\rangle
by

|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc},t}\rangle:=\Gamma_{-}(\alpha v)\Gamma^{\prime}_{-}(-v)|\underline{v,t}\rangle,

(3.71)

i.e. we modify the fermionic propagators rather than the boundary
specialization
\rho_{N}^{-}
.

Of course, we could perform a similar trick to introduce instead an
extra weight
\beta^{or}
counting the number of odd rows of

\mu^{(N)}
. Let us record the corresponding redefinition of the

\kappa
’s for
u=0
and
v=1
:

\displaystyle\kappa_{1,1}(z,w)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(z-w)}{(zw-1)(z+\beta)(w+\beta)},\qquad

\displaystyle\kappa_{1,2}(z,w)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(zw-1)(w+\beta)}{(z-w)(w^{2}-1)(z+\beta)},

(3.72)

\displaystyle\kappa_{2,2}(z,w)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(z-w)(z+\beta)(w+\beta)}{(z^{2}-1)(w^{2}-1)(zw-1)}.

For bookkeeping purposes, we gather in
Appendix
C
the expressions for all
modified fermionic propagators with one free boundary state. The case
of two free boundaries is left as an exercise to the reader.

3.3.3
General correlation functions

It turns out that the free fermion formalism used in this section
allows to derive an explicit
2n
-fold contour integral representation
for the general
n
-point correlation function of both

\mathfrak{S}(\vec{\lambda})
and
\mathfrak{S}_{t}(\vec{\lambda})
.

Theorem 3.14
.

Let
U=\{(i_{1},k_{1}),\ldots,(i_{n},k_{n})\}
be a finite subset of

\{1,\ldots,N\}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}
, with
i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{n}
. The
probability
\varrho(U)
that the point process

\mathfrak{S}(\vec{\lambda})
contains
U
reads

\varrho(U)=\frac{1}{(2\mathrm{i}\pi)^{2n}}\oint\cdots\oint\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{j}\mathrm{d}w_{j}}{z_{j}^{k_{j}+1}w_{j}^{-k_{j}+1}}\cdot\frac{F(i_{j},z_{j})}{F(i_{j},w_{j})}\right)\Phi(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n};w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})

(3.73)

where the contour integrals are taken over
2n
nested circles

\min(R,u^{-1})>|z_{1}|>|w_{1}|>\cdots>|z_{n}|>|w_{n}|>\max(v,R^{-1})
,
and where
F
is as in
Theorem
2.5
while

\Phi(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n};w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})=\sqrt{\frac{w_{1}\cdots w_{n}}{z_{1}\cdots z_{n}}}\frac{((uv)^{2};(uv)^{2})_{\infty}^{2n}}{\prod_{i=1}^{n}(uz_{i},-uw_{i},-vz_{i}^{-1},vw_{i}^{-1};uv)_{\infty}}\\
\times\frac{\prod_{i,j=1}^{n}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(u^{2}z_{i}w_{j})}{\prod_{1\leq i\leq j\leq n}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(w_{j}/z_{i})\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(z_{j}/w_{i})}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\frac{\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(z_{j}/z_{i})\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(w_{j}/w_{i})}{\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(u^{2}z_{i}z_{j})\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(u^{2}w_{i}w_{j})}.

(3.74)

The probability
\varrho_{t}(U)
that the point process

\mathfrak{S}_{t}(\vec{\lambda})
contains
U
admits the same
expression, upon replacing
\Phi(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n};w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})

by

\Phi_{t}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n};w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})=\theta_{3}\left(\left(t\frac{z_{1}\cdots z_{n}}{w_{1}\cdots w_{n}}\right)^{2};(uv)^{4}\right)\frac{\Phi(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n};w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})}{\theta_{3}(t^{2};(uv)^{4})}.

(3.75)

Proof.

We start from the fermionic representation (
3.26
) of

\varrho(U)
, and plug in the contour integral representation
(
3.61
) of
\Psi_{k}(i)
and
\Psi^{*}_{k}(i)
: we obtain the

2n
-fold contour integral (
3.73
) with

\Phi(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n};w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})=\frac{\langle\underline{u}|\psi(z_{1})\psi^{*}(w_{1})\cdots\psi(z_{n})\psi^{*}(w_{n})|\underline{v}\rangle}{\langle\underline{u}|\underline{v}\rangle}.

(3.76)

This quantity may be evaluated by the same strategy as for the
fermionic propagators in Section
3.3.1
, by using the
boson–fermion correspondence (Proposition
3.2
)
to rewrite the
\psi/\psi^{*}
in terms of vertex operators (and
R

and
C
operators that are immediately factored out). We recognize
the partition function of a certain free boundary Schur process of
length
2N
, which after some massaging yields (
3.74
).
The discussion of integration contours is easily adapted from that
in Section
3.3.1
.

For
\varrho_{t}(U)
, we proceed in the same way starting from the
fermionic representation (
3.57
), which amounts to
replacing
\Phi
by

\Phi_{t}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n};w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})=\frac{\langle\underline{u,t}|\psi(z_{1})\psi^{*}(w_{1})\cdots\psi(z_{n})\psi^{*}(w_{n})|\underline{v,t}\rangle}{\langle\underline{u,t}|\underline{v,t}\rangle}.

(3.77)

By (
3.39
) and (
3.40
), we see that
\Phi_{t}

differs from
\Phi
by a simple charge-related factor, leading to
(
3.75
).
∎

Remark 3.15
.

By Wick’s lemma for two free boundaries (Lemma
3.10
),
we have

\Phi_{t}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n};w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})=\pf\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\kappa_{1,1}(z_{i},z_{j})&\kappa_{1,2}(z_{i},w_{j})\\
-\kappa_{1,2}(z_{j},w_{i})&\kappa_{2,2}(w_{i},w_{j})\\
\end{array}\right]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}

(3.78)

where the
\kappa
’s are as in Theorem
2.5
. Plugging
in the explicit expression for
\Phi_{t}
given in
Theorem
3.14
, we obtain a remarkable pfaffian identity
which amounts to a particular case of an identity due to Okada

[
Oka06
]
. In the case
u=v=0
, this identity reduces to the
well-known Cauchy determinant. In the case
u=0
,
v=1
of
Theorem
2.2
, we obtain an identity equivalent to
Schur’s pfaffian

\pf_{1\leq i<j\leq 2n}\frac{x_{i}-x_{j}}{x_{i}+x_{j}}=\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq 2n}\frac{x_{i}-x_{j}}{x_{i}+x_{j}}
(the equivalence goes as
follows: substitute the expression (
2.10
) for the

\kappa
’s, pull out the trivial row/column factors and take

x_{2i-1}=\frac{z_{i}-1}{z_{i}+1}
,
x_{2i}=\frac{1-w_{i}}{w_{i}+1}
for

i=1,\ldots,n
).

4
Symmetric Last Passage Percolation

In this section we consider the last passage percolation (LPP) time with symmetric and up to symmetry independent geometric weights. For
(a_{n})_{n\geq 1}\in(0,1)^{\mathbb{N}},

\alpha\in(0,\inf\{\frac{1}{a_{n}},n\geq 1\})
and
r,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
these weights are given by

\omega_{r,t}=\omega_{t,r}\sim\begin{cases}\vskip 8.5359ptg(a_{r}a_{t}),\quad&\mathrm{if}\quad r\neq t,\\
g(\alpha a_{r}),\quad&\mathrm{if}\quad r=t\end{cases}

(4.1)

where
\Prob(g(q)=k)=q^{k}(1-q)
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

For
(k,l),(m,n)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 1}
with
k\leq m,l\leq n
, consider up-right paths
\pi
from
(k,l)
to
(m,n)
, i.e.
\pi=(\pi(0),\pi(1),\ldots,\pi(m-k+n-l))
with
\pi(0)=(k,l),\pi(m-k+n-l)=(m,n)
and

\pi(i)-\pi(i-1)\in\{(0,1),(1,0)\}.
The symmetric LPP time with geometric weights (
4.1
) is then defined to be

L_{(k,l)\to(m,n)}:=\max_{\pi:(k,l)\to(m,n)}\sum_{(r,t)\in\pi}\omega_{r,t}

(4.2)

where the maximum is taken over all up-right paths from
(k,l)
to
(m,n)
. Note that we have the recursion

L_{(k,l)\to(m,n)}=\max\{L_{(k,l)\to(m-1,n)},L_{(k,l)\to(m,n-1)}\}+\omega_{m,n}.

(4.3)

4.1
Definition of distribution functions

We start by defining the distribution functions which will appear later. The distributions defined below are mostly given in terms of contour integrals, which is why we make the following definition.
For
\varphi\in[0,2\pi]
and
z\in\mathbb{R}

denote by
\mathfrak{G}_{z}^{\varphi}=\{z+|s|e^{\mathrm{sgn}(s)\mathrm{i}\varphi},s\in\mathbb{R}\}
the infinite curve oriented from
z+\infty e^{-\mathrm{i}\varphi}
to
z+\infty e^{\mathrm{i}\varphi}
. If
f
is a function and
V\subset\mathbb{C}
, we denote by
\gamma_{V}
any counterclockwise oriented simple closed curve containing all elements of
V
in its interior and excluding all poles of
f
that are not elements of
V
.

Let
u_{1}>u_{2}>\cdots>u_{k}\geq 0
and
a,b\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
. For
v\in\mathbb{R}
we define

K_{1,1}^{v}(u_{a},\xi;u_{b},\xi^{\prime})=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{1}^{\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{1}^{\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}W\frac{(Z-W)(W+2v)(Z+2v)}{4ZW(Z+W)}\frac{e^{Z^{3}/3-Z^{2}u_{a}-Z\xi}}{e^{-W^{3}/3+W^{2}u_{b}+W\xi^{\prime}}}

(4.4)

and
K_{1,2}^{v}=K_{1,2}^{v,1}+K_{1,2}^{v,2},
where

\displaystyle K_{1,2}^{v,1}(u_{a},\xi;u_{b},\xi^{\prime})=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{A_{Z}}^{\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{A_{W}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}W\frac{(Z+W)(Z+2v)}{2(W+2v)Z(Z-W)}\frac{e^{Z^{3}/3-Z^{2}u_{a}-\xi Z}}{e^{W^{3}/3-W^{2}u_{b}-W\xi^{\prime}}},

(4.5)

with
A_{Z}>A_{W}>-2v,A_{Z}>0
. For
a\leq b,
we have
K_{1,2}^{v,2}(u_{a},\xi;u_{b},\xi^{\prime})=0,
and if
a>b
, then

\displaystyle K_{1,2}^{v,2}(u_{a},\xi;u_{b},\xi^{\prime})=\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}Ze^{Z^{2}(u_{b}-u_{a})+Z(\xi^{\prime}-\xi)}

(4.6)

with
\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}
oriented with increasing imaginary part.
Finally, we define
K_{2,2}^{v}=K_{2,2}^{v,1}+K_{2,2}^{v,2}
through

\displaystyle K_{2,2}^{v,1}(u_{a},\xi;u_{b},\xi^{\prime})=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{B_{3}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{B_{4}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}W\frac{Z-W}{(W+2v)(Z+2v)(Z+W)}\frac{e^{-Z^{3}/3+u_{a}Z^{2}+Z\xi}}{e^{W^{3}/3-u_{b}W^{2}-W\xi^{\prime}}},

(4.7)

with
B_{3}>-2v>B_{4},B_{3}<-B_{4}
.

We define
K_{2,2}^{v,2}
for
u_{a}=u_{b}=0
to be

K_{2,2}^{v,2}(0,\xi;0,\xi^{\prime})=\frac{e^{8v^{3}/3-2v\xi^{\prime}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{C_{1}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Z\frac{e^{-Z^{3}/3+Z\xi}}{Z-2v}+\mathrm{sgn}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi)e^{-2v|\xi-\xi^{\prime}|}

(4.8)

with
C_{1}<2v,
whereas if
u_{a}+u_{b}>0

\displaystyle\begin{split}K_{2,2}^{v,2}(u_{a},\xi;u_{b},\xi^{\prime})&=\frac{e^{8v^{3}/3+4v^{2}u_{b}-2v\xi^{\prime}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{B_{2}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Z\frac{1}{Z-2v}e^{-Z^{3}/3+Z^{2}u_{a}+\xi Z}\\
&-\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{2\pi/3}_{B_{1}}}\mathrm{d}Z\frac{2Z}{(Z+2v)(Z-2v)}e^{Z^{2}(u_{a}+u_{b})+Z(\xi-\xi^{\prime})}\end{split}

(4.9)

with
B_{1}>2|v|,

B_{2}>2v
.
We can now define the following antisymmetric kernel, note that we introduce the prefactor
d_{q}=\frac{q^{1/6}}{2(1+\sqrt{q})^{2/3}}
in its definition so we do not have to insert it later:

\displaystyle K^{v}(u_{a},\xi;u_{b},\xi^{\prime})=\begin{pmatrix}[l]K_{1,1}^{v}(d_{q}u_{a},\xi;d_{q}u_{b},\xi^{\prime})&K_{1,2}^{v}(d_{q}u_{a},\xi;d_{q}u_{b},\xi^{\prime})\\
-K_{1,2}^{v}(d_{q}u_{b},\xi^{\prime};d_{q}u_{a},\xi)&K_{2,2}^{v}(d_{q}u_{a},\xi;d_{q}u_{b},\xi^{\prime})\end{pmatrix}.

(4.10)

The Tracy–Widom GUE distribution is given by

\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{GUE}}(s)=\pf(J-\hat{K}_{\mathcal{A}_{2}})_{(s,\infty)}

(4.11)

with
\hat{K}_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}(x,y)=\begin{pmatrix}[l]0&K_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}(x,y)\\
-K_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}(y,x)&0\end{pmatrix}

and

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}(x,y)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{2\pi/3}_{-1}}\mathrm{d}z\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{\pi/3}_{1}}\mathrm{d}w\frac{e^{w^{3}-wx}}{e^{z^{3}-zy}}\frac{1}{w-z}

(4.12)

is the Airy kernel.
We define
F_{u,v}
through

\displaystyle F_{u,v}(s)=\pf(J-K^{v}(u,\xi;u,\xi^{\prime}))_{(s,\infty)}.

(4.13)

The
F_{\mathrm{GOE}}
and
F_{\mathrm{GSE}}
distributions which appear in the following can be defined through Fredholm pfaffians — see e.g. Lemmas 2.6, 2.7 in
[
BBCS18
]
, but their explicit form will not be needed later and hence we omit giving it.
F_{u,v}
interpolates between various distribution functions. First, we have
F_{0,0}(s)=F_{\mathrm{GOE}}(s)
; the equivalence of
F_{0,0}
with existing definitions of
F_{\mathrm{GOE}}
was checked in e.g. Lemma 2.6 of
[
BBCS18
]
. It follows from our Theorem
4.2
and (4.26) of
[
BR99
]
that
F_{0,v}(s)=F^{\boxslash}(s;v)
, where
F^{\boxslash}
is defined in Definition 4 of
[
BR01b
]
. This and (2.33) in
[
BR01b
]
imply
\lim_{v\to+\infty}F_{0,v}(s)=F_{\mathrm{GSE}}(s).
Finally, if
v=v(u)
is such that
u+2v(u)\to+\infty
for
u\to+\infty,

then
\lim_{u\to+\infty}F_{u,v}(s-u^{2}d_{q}^{2})=F_{\mathrm{GUE}}(s)
(with
d_{q}=\frac{q^{1/6}}{2(1+\sqrt{q})^{2/3}}
). This follows from the convergence of
K^{v}
(under conjugation) to
\hat{K}_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}
and dominated convergence.

4.2
Results and proofs

The first result we present is a formula for the multipoint distribution of LPP times along down-right paths.

Theorem 4.1
.

Consider the LPP time (
4.2
) with weights (
4.1
).
Let
r_{l},t_{l}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1},r_{l}\leq t_{l},l=1,\ldots,k
with
r_{1}\leq\ldots\leq r_{k},t_{1}\geq\ldots\geq t_{k}.
Then

\displaystyle\Prob\left(\bigcap_{l=1}^{k}\{L_{(1,1)\to(r_{l},t_{l})}\leq s_{l}\}\right)=\pf(J-K)_{B}

(4.14)

where
B=\{(i,x)\in\{1,\ldots,k\}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\prime}:x>s_{i}-1/2\}
is equipped with the counting measure. The kernel
K
is given by

\begin{split}K_{1,1}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=&\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint\mathrm{d}z\oint\mathrm{d}w\frac{z-w}{(z^{2}-1)(w^{2}-1)(zw-1)}z^{-x_{a}+1/2}w^{-x_{b}+1/2}(1-\alpha/z)(1-\alpha/w)\\
&\times\prod_{i=1}^{r_{a}}\frac{1}{1-a_{i}z}\prod_{i=1}^{r_{b}}\frac{1}{1-a_{i}w}\prod_{i=1}^{t_{a}}(1-a_{i}/z)\prod_{i=1}^{t_{b}}(1-a_{i}/w)\end{split}

(4.15)

for counterclockwise oriented circle contours around
0
satisfying
\alpha,1<|z|,|w|<\min_{i=1,\ldots,t_{1}}\frac{1}{a_{i}}
;

\begin{split}K_{1,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=&\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint\mathrm{d}z\oint\mathrm{d}w\frac{zw-1}{(z-w)(z^{2}-1)}z^{-x_{a}+1/2}w^{x_{b}-3/2}\frac{1-\alpha/z}{1-\alpha/w}\\
&\times\prod_{i=1}^{r_{a}}\frac{1}{1-a_{i}z}\prod_{i=1}^{r_{b}}(1-a_{i}w)\prod_{i=1}^{t_{a}}(1-a_{i}/z)\prod_{i=1}^{t_{b}}\frac{1}{1-a_{i}/w}\end{split}

(4.16)

for counterclockwise oriented circle contours around
0
with
\max_{i=1,\ldots,t_{1}}a_{i},\alpha<|z|,|w|<\min_{i=1,\ldots,t_{1}}\frac{1}{a_{i}}
and, if
a\leq b
,
|w|<|z|>1,

and, if
b<a
,
1<|z|<|w|,
; and finally

\begin{split}K_{2,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=&\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint\mathrm{d}z\oint\mathrm{d}wz^{x_{a}-3/2}w^{x_{b}-3/2}\frac{z-w}{zw-1}\frac{1}{(1-\alpha/z)(1-\alpha/w)}\\
&\times\prod_{i=1}^{r_{a}}(1-a_{i}z)\prod_{i=1}^{r_{b}}(1-a_{i}w)\prod_{i=1}^{t_{a}}\frac{1}{1-a_{i}/z}\prod_{i=1}^{t_{b}}\frac{1}{1-a_{i}/w}\end{split}

(4.17)

for counterclockwise oriented circle contours around
0
with
\alpha,\max_{i=1,\ldots,t_{1}}a_{i}<|z|,|w|<\min_{i=1,\ldots,t_{1}}\frac{1}{a_{i}}
and
1<|zw|.

The following theorem will be obtained from the previous one by asymptotic analysis.

Theorem 4.2
.

Consider the weights (
4.1
) with
a_{j}=\sqrt{q},q\in(0,1),j\geq 1
and
\alpha=1-2vc_{q}N^{-1/3},
where
c_{q}=\frac{1-\sqrt{q}}{q^{1/6}(1+\sqrt{q})^{1/3}},v\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
u_{1}>\cdots>u_{k}\geq 0
.
Then

\begin{split}\lim_{N\to\infty}\Prob\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^{k}\big\{L_{(1,1)\to(N-\lfloor u_{i}N^{2/3}\rfloor,N)}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{q}N}{1-\sqrt{q}}-u_{i}\frac{\sqrt{q}N^{2/3}}{1-\sqrt{q}}+c_{q}^{-1}s_{i}N^{1/3}\big\}\right)=\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad=\pf(J-\chi_{s}K^{v}\chi_{s})_{\{u_{1},\ldots,u_{k}\}\times\mathbb{R}}\end{split}

(4.18)

where
\chi_{s}(u_{i},x)=\mathbf{1}_{x>s_{i}}
and
K^{v}
is defined in (
4.10
).

Remark 4.3
.

As was already mentioned, further asymptotic regimes can be considered. The geometric LPP time
L_{(1,1)\to(\kappa N,N)}
with weights (
4.1
) and
a_{n}=\sqrt{q}
converges to
F_{\mathrm{GUE}}
as long as
\alpha<\frac{1+\sqrt{\kappa q}}{\sqrt{\kappa}+\sqrt{q}}
. Also, as was already obtained in
[
BR99
]
,
L_{(1,1)\to(N,N)}
(rescaled) converges to
F_{\mathrm{GSE}}
for
\alpha<1
. We note that this case is more involved than the others, see Section 5 of
[
BBCS18
]
for a discussion and solution of the arising difficulties in the exponential case. Finally, the law of large numbers limit of
L_{(1,1)\to(N,N)}
changes for
\alpha>1
and
L_{(1,1)\to(N,N)}
converges to a Gaussian random variable under
N^{1/2}
scaling.

4.2.1
Proofs of Theorems
4.1
and
4.2

We start by proving Theorem
4.1
. The symmetric LPP time (
4.2
) is a marginal of a Schur process with an
even columns
free boundary partition. This can be seen using the framework developed in
[
BBB+18
]
which we mostly follow and refer to for further references. For a word
w=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})\in\{\prec,\succ\}^{n}
and a sequence of partitions
\vec{\lambda}=(\emptyset=\lambda^{(0)},\ldots,\lambda^{(n)})
we say that
\vec{\lambda}
is
w-
interlaced if
\lambda^{(i-1)}w_{i}\lambda^{(i)},i=1,\ldots,n
and we define

w^{\star}=(w^{\star}_{n},\ldots,w^{\star}_{1})\in\{\prec,\succ\}^{n}
by imposing
w^{\star}_{i}\neq w_{i}
. Furthermore, given
w
, we set
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma_{+}
(resp.
\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma_{-}
) if
w_{i}
equals
\prec
(resp.
\succ
), and we define
w^{\mathrm{sym}}=w\cdot w^{\star}
where
\cdot
means concatenation. For
w\in\{\prec,\succ\}^{n},
we label the elements of
\{i:\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma_{+}\}

as
i_{1}\leq\ldots\leq i_{m}
and those of
\{i:\Gamma_{i}=\Gamma_{-}\}
as
j_{n-m}\leq\ldots\leq j_{1}.
We now define
\mathfrak{s}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{s}_{n}
by setting

\mathfrak{s}_{i_{k}}=a_{k},\quad k=1,\ldots,m;\quad\mathfrak{s}_{j_{k}}=a_{k},\quad k=1,\ldots,n-m

(4.19)

with the
(a_{n})_{n\geq 1}
from (
4.1
).

To
w
we associate an encoded shape: we construct a down-right path
\hat{\pi}=(\hat{\pi}(0),\ldots,\hat{\pi}(n)),\hat{\pi}(0)=(1,\#\{i:w_{i}=\succ\}+1),\hat{\pi}(n)=(\#\{i:w_{i}=\prec\}+1,1)
of unit steps by setting
\hat{\pi}(i+1)-\hat{\pi}(i)=(1,0)
if
w_{i+1}
equals
\prec
, and
\hat{\pi}(i+1)-\hat{\pi}(i)=(0,-1)
otherwise. This path can be seen as the boundary of a Young diagram drawn in French convention, denoted by
sh(w)
; the bottom left corner of this Young diagram is located at
(1,1)
— see Figure
7
left. For fixed
\lambda,\mu
we have the bijective local growth rule

\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{loc}}:\{\kappa:\kappa\prec\lambda,\kappa\prec\mu\}\times\mathbb{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\to\{\nu:\nu\succ\lambda,\nu\succ\mu\}

(4.20)

where
\nu:=\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{loc}}((\kappa,k))
is given by

\begin{split}\nu_{1}&=\max\{\lambda_{1},\mu_{1}\}+k,\\
\nu_{i}&=\max\{\lambda_{i},\mu_{i}\}+\min\{\lambda_{i-1},\mu_{i-1}\}-\kappa_{i-1},\quad i\geq 2\end{split}

(4.21)

and one has

|\nu|+|\kappa|=|\mu|+|\lambda|+k.

(4.22)

Let
\{G_{r,t},r,t\geq 1\}
be nonnegative integers with
G_{r,t}=G_{t,r}
(
G_{r,t}
is a possible realization of the
\omega_{r,t}
from (
4.1
)). We now recursively construct partitions
\lambda^{r,t},(r,t)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}^{2}
as follows: for
(r,t)\in\{(1,k):k\geq 1\}\cup\{(k,1):k\geq 1\}
we set
\lambda^{r,t}=\emptyset
. Given
\lambda^{r,t}=:\kappa,\lambda^{r+1,t}=:\mu,\lambda^{r,t+1}=:\lambda
we define

\displaystyle\lambda^{r+1,t+1}:=\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{loc}}((\kappa,G_{r,t})).

(4.23)

Note that, since
G_{r,t}=G_{t,r}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{loc}}
is symmetric in
\lambda
and
\mu
, we have that
\lambda^{r,t}=\lambda^{t,r}
.

Given
w^{\mathrm{sym}}\in\{\prec,\succ\}^{2n}
and the corresponding down-right path
\hat{\pi}=(\hat{\pi}(0),\ldots,\hat{\pi}(2n))
, we denote

\displaystyle\lambda^{(m)}=\lambda^{\hat{\pi}(m)},m=0,\ldots,2n

(4.24)

and note that we have
\lambda^{(n+k)}=\lambda^{(n-k)},k=0,\ldots,n
— see Figure
7
right.

\hat{\pi}
\prec
\succ
\prec
\succ
\prec
\succ
(1,1)
G_{1,1}
G_{1,2}
G_{1,3}
G_{2,2}
G_{1,2}
G_{1,3}
\emptyset
\emptyset
\lambda^{(0)}=\emptyset
\emptyset
\emptyset
\emptyset
\emptyset=\lambda^{(0)}
\lambda^{(1)}
\lambda^{(2)}
\lambda^{(2)}
\lambda^{(3)}
\lambda^{(1)}

Figure 7
:
Left: the encoded shape
sh(w^{\mathrm{sym}})=(3,2,1)
for
w=(\prec,\succ,\prec)
together with the down-right path
\hat{\pi}
in bold; right:
the partitions
\lambda^{(0)},\ldots,\lambda^{(3)},\ldots,\lambda^{(0)}
associated to the points of
\hat{\pi}
are constructed recursively using the empty partitions on the boundary, the
G_{r,t}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{loc}}
.

The following proposition is an elementary induction on
|sh(w^{\mathrm{sym}})|
(see Theorem 3.2 of
[
BBB+18
]
for a similar proof), which we omit carrying out (the partition function
Z_{w}
appearing in (
4.26
) was computed in (
2.6
)).

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
w\in\{\prec,\succ\}^{n},w^{\mathrm{sym}}=w\cdot w^{\star}
and
\mathfrak{s}_{i},i=1,\ldots,2n,
be the variables (
4.19
) for
w^{\mathrm{sym}}
. With the symmetric weights (
4.1
) we obtain by (
4.24
) a probability distribution on

\{\vec{\lambda}:\vec{\lambda}=(\emptyset=\lambda^{(0)},\ldots,\lambda^{(n-1)},\lambda^{(n)},\lambda^{(n-1)},\ldots,\lambda^{(0)}),\vec{\lambda}\text{\,\,is\,\,}w^{\mathrm{sym}}\text{-interlaced\,}\}

(4.25)

which is given by

\displaystyle\Prob(\{\vec{\lambda}\})=\frac{1}{Z_{w}}\langle\lambda^{(n)}|\Gamma_{-}(\{\alpha\})|\underline{1}_{ec}\rangle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle\lambda^{(i-1)}|\Gamma_{i}(\{\mathfrak{s}_{i}\})|\lambda^{(i)}\rangle.

(4.26)

Now we can prove Theorem
4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Note that, by construction,
L_{(1,1)\to(m,n)}=\lambda^{m+1,n+1}_{1}
, where
\lambda^{m+1,n+1}_{1}
is from (
4.23
) and
G_{r,t}=\omega_{r,t}
. Consequently, the LPP times become a gap probability for the point process
\mathfrak{S}(\vec{\lambda})
where
\vec{\lambda}
is distributed as (
4.26
). By (
A.11
), the left hand side of (
4.14
) is given as a Fredholm pfaffian, and the corresponding correlation functions were computed in Theorem
2.2
and (
2.11
), leading to the identity (
4.14
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

We have to show the convergence, as
N\to\infty
, of the Fredholm pfaffian provided by Theorem
4.1
. By Proposition
4.5
, the kernel
K
from Theorem
4.1
converges pointwise to
K^{v},
and by Proposition
4.6
we can apply Lemma
A.3
which yields an integrable upper bound, allowing us to apply dominated convergence to show the convergence of the Fredholm pfaffian.
∎

4.2.2
Asymptotics

In the following asymptotics, we will ignore the integer parts in (
4.27
) when it leads to no confusion.

Proposition 4.5
.

Set
a_{j}=\sqrt{q}
for a
q\in(0,1)
and let
u_{1}>\cdots>u_{k}\geq 0
and
c_{q}=\frac{1-\sqrt{q}}{q^{1/6}(1+\sqrt{q})^{1/3}},d_{q}=\frac{q^{1/6}}{2(1+\sqrt{q})^{2/3}}
. Set
\alpha=1-2vc_{q}N^{-1/3},v\in\mathbb{R}.
We take
t_{l}=N,r_{l}=N-\lfloor u_{l}N^{2/3}\rfloor,l=1,\ldots,k
and

\begin{split}x_{a}&=\lfloor\frac{2\sqrt{q}}{1-\sqrt{q}}N\rfloor-\lfloor u_{a}N^{2/3}\frac{\sqrt{q}}{1-\sqrt{q}}\rfloor+\lfloor\xi N^{1/3}\rfloor-1/2,\\
x_{b}&=\lfloor\frac{2\sqrt{q}}{1-\sqrt{q}}N\rfloor-\lfloor u_{b}N^{2/3}\frac{\sqrt{q}}{1-\sqrt{q}}\rfloor+\lfloor\xi^{\prime}N^{1/3}\rfloor-1/2.\end{split}

(4.27)

We then have for the kernels
K
from Theorem
4.1
and
K^{v}
from (
4.10
):

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{2/3}e^{-N^{2/3}\ln(1-\sqrt{q})(u_{a}+u_{b})}c_{q}^{-1}K_{1,1}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=c_{q}K_{1,1}^{v}(d_{q}u_{a},c_{q}\xi;d_{q}u_{b},c_{q}\xi^{\prime}),\\
&\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{1/3}e^{N^{2/3}\ln(1-\sqrt{q})(u_{b}-u_{a})}K_{1,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=c_{q}K_{1,2}^{v}(d_{q}u_{a},c_{q}\xi;d_{q}u_{b},c_{q}\xi^{\prime}),\\
&\lim_{N\to\infty}e^{N^{2/3}\ln(1-\sqrt{q})(u_{b}+u_{a})}c_{q}K_{2,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=c_{q}K_{2,2}^{v}(d_{q}u_{a},c_{q}\xi;d_{q}u_{b},c_{q}\xi^{\prime}).\end{split}

(4.28)

Proof.

We start with
K_{1,2}
. We get for
a\leq b

\displaystyle K_{1,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint_{1/\sqrt{q}>|z|>\alpha,1}\mathrm{d}z\oint_{1/\sqrt{q},|z|>|w|>\alpha,\sqrt{q}}\mathrm{d}w\frac{z-\alpha}{w-\alpha}\frac{zw-1}{w(z-w)(z-1)(z+1)}

(4.29)

\displaystyle\times\frac{e^{Nf_{1}(z)+N^{2/3}u_{a}f_{2}(z)-N^{1/3}f_{3}(z)\xi}}{e^{Nf_{1}(w)+N^{2/3}u_{b}f_{2}(w)-N^{1/3}f_{3}(w)\xi^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}}\mathrm{d}z\oint_{\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}}\mathrm{d}w\frac{z-\alpha}{w-\alpha}\frac{zw-1}{w(z-w)(z-1)(z+1)}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\times\frac{e^{Nf_{1}(z)+N^{2/3}u_{a}f_{2}(z)-N^{1/3}f_{3}(z)\xi}}{e^{Nf_{1}(w)+N^{2/3}u_{b}f_{2}(w)-N^{1/3}f_{3}(w)\xi^{\prime}}}

where we have set

\displaystyle f_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\ln(1-\sqrt{q}/z)-\ln(1-\sqrt{q}z)-\ln(z)\frac{2\sqrt{q}}{1-\sqrt{q}},

(4.31)

\displaystyle f_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\ln(z)\frac{\sqrt{q}}{1-\sqrt{q}}+\ln(1-\sqrt{q}z),

\displaystyle f_{3}(z)

\displaystyle=\ln(z).

One readily computes
f_{1}(1)=f_{1}^{\prime}(1)=f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(1)=0,f_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}(1)=2c_{q}^{-3}
and
f_{2}(1)=\ln(1-\sqrt{q}),f_{2}^{\prime}(1)=0,f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(1)=-\frac{\sqrt{q}}{(1-\sqrt{q})^{2}}
, also
d_{q}=-f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)c_{q}^{2}/2
.

Let us briefly outline the strategy for the asymptotics. We choose contours
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}
for
z
and
\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}
for
w
such that they pass (almost) through the critical point
1
. Furthermore, for
\delta>0
small (but independent of
N
) we want
\Re(f_{1}(z))\leq-c_{0,0}
and
-\Re(f_{1}(w))\leq-c_{0,0}
for some
c_{0,0}>0
(depending on
\delta
) for
z\in\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta},w\in\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}
where we define
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}=\{z\in\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}:|z-1|\leq\delta\},\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}=\{w\in\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}:|w-1|\leq\delta\}
.

Given this, the integral over
(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}\cup\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta})^{c}
will vanish as
N\to\infty
. On
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}\cup\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}
we use Taylor to obtain
Nf_{1}(1+Zc_{q}N^{-1/3})=Z^{3}/3+\mathcal{O}(Z^{4}N^{-1/3})
. For this reason we want
(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}-1)N^{1/3}
respectively
(\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}-1)N^{1/3}
to lie (up to a part of length
\mathcal{O}(1)
) in
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\Re(z^{3})<0\}
respectively
\{w\in\mathbb{C}:\Re(w^{3})>0\}.

We first note that
\Re(f_{1}(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))=0
for all
s
. Furthermore, we compute

L(z)=zf_{1}^{\prime}(z)=\frac{\sqrt{q}(1+\sqrt{q})}{1-\sqrt{q}}\frac{(1-z)^{2}}{(\sqrt{q}-z)(-1+\sqrt{q}z)}.

(4.32)

An elementary computation shows

\Im(L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))=0.

(4.33)

Next we treat
\Re(L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))=L(e^{\mathrm{i}s})
. We have

\Re(L(z))=\frac{\sqrt{q}(1+\sqrt{q})}{1-\sqrt{q}}\frac{1}{|(\sqrt{q}-z)(-1+\sqrt{q}z)|^{2}}\Re((1-z)^{2}(\sqrt{q}-\bar{z})(-1+\sqrt{q}\bar{z})).

(4.34)

This implies by a simple computation

L(e^{\mathrm{i}s})=\frac{\sqrt{q}(1+\sqrt{q})}{1-\sqrt{q}}\frac{2(-1+\cos(s))}{|\sqrt{q}-e^{\mathrm{i}s}|^{2}}.

(4.35)

Let now
\varepsilon>0,
and
\phi\in[0,2\pi]
. Then, by Taylor approximation for some
t_{s}\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\Re(f_{1}(e^{\mathrm{i}s}+\varepsilon e^{\mathrm{i}\phi}))

\displaystyle=\Re(f_{1}(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))+\Re(f_{1}^{\prime}(e^{\mathrm{i}s})\varepsilon e^{\mathrm{i}\phi})+\frac{1}{2}\Re(f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(e^{\mathrm{i}s}+t_{s}\varepsilon e^{\mathsf{i}\phi})\varepsilon^{2}e^{2\mathrm{i}\phi})

(4.36)

\displaystyle=L(e^{\mathrm{i}s})\varepsilon\cos(\phi-s)+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\Re(f_{1}^{\prime\prime}(e^{\mathrm{i}s}+t_{s}\varepsilon e^{\mathrm{i}\phi})e^{2\mathrm{i}\phi}).

Take
\varepsilon=-\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s})
for
\varepsilon_{1}>0
small
and
\phi=\pi+s
respectively
\phi=s
to get

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\Re(f_{1}((1+\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))e^{\mathrm{i}s}))\geq L(e^{\mathrm{i}s})^{2}\varepsilon_{1}/2,

(4.37)

\displaystyle\Re(f_{1}((1-\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))e^{\mathrm{i}s}))\leq-L(e^{\mathrm{i}s})^{2}\varepsilon_{1}/2.

Choose now the contour (Figure
8
)

\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}(s)=(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2))(1+\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))e^{\mathrm{i}s},\quad s\in[0,2\pi].

(4.38)

The prefactor
(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2))
makes sure that
\alpha
lies inside the contour. Furthermore, by (
4.37
) and (
4.35
), for any
\delta>0
independent of
N
there is a
c_{0,0}>0
for which we have
-\Re(f_{1}(w))\leq-c_{0,0}
for
w\in\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}\setminus\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}=\{w\in\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}:|w-1|>\delta\}.
To choose
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}
consider first

\tilde{\gamma}_{-1,0,1,w}(s)=(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1))(1-\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))e^{\mathrm{i}s},\quad s\in[0,2\pi].

(4.39)

By (
4.37
) and (
4.35
),
\Re(f_{1}(\tilde{\gamma}_{-1,0,1,w}(s)))
behaves as desired, but
\Re(\tilde{\gamma}_{-1,0,1,w}(s))<1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1))
if
\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s})(-1+\cos(s))^{-1}
is too small, i.e. the contour may not cross the positive real axis with the right angles. We thus do a local modification. Choose
\eta_{1}>0
small and let
\eta_{2}>0
be the number such that
e^{\mathrm{i}\arcsin(\eta_{1})}+\eta_{2}\in\{(1-\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))e^{\mathrm{i}s},s\in[0,2\pi]\}
, and let
e^{\mathrm{i}\arcsin(\eta_{1})}+\eta_{2}=(1-\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s_{\eta_{2}}}))e^{\mathrm{i}s_{\eta_{2}}}
. Let
M>1
and define

\begin{split}&\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{loc,1}(s)=1+\mathrm{i}s+s/M,\,s\in[0,\eta_{1}]\mathrm{\quad and}\\
&\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{loc,2}(s)=e^{\mathrm{i}\arcsin(\eta_{1})}+s,\,s\in[\eta_{2},1+\eta_{1}/M-\cos(\arcsin(\eta_{1}))].\end{split}

(4.40)

Choosing
M
sufficiently large and
\eta_{1}
sufficiently small we get by Taylor approximation around
1
and
e^{\arcsin(\eta_{1})\mathrm{i}}
that for some constants
d_{1},d_{2}>0

\displaystyle\Re(f_{1}(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{loc,1}(s)))\leq-s^{3}d_{1}\mathrm{\quad and\quad}\Re(f_{1}(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{loc,2}(s)))\leq-\eta_{1}^{2}sd_{2}.

(4.41)

Define finally
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{loc,3}=\{(1-\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))e^{\mathrm{i}s},s\in[s_{\eta_{2}},\pi]\}
.

Since
\Re(f_{1}(z))=\Re(f_{1}(\bar{z}))
it suffices to define
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}
on the upper half plane. We set

\displaystyle\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}=(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1))(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{loc,1}+\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{loc,2}+\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{loc,3})

(4.42)

where the
+
means we concatenate the curves such that
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}
is counterclockwise oriented. On the lower half plane, we simply take the image of (
4.42
) under complex conjugation. We now choose
\delta<\eta_{1}
such that
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}\subset\{1+\mathrm{i}s+|s|/M,s\in[-\eta_{1},\eta_{1}]\}
.

1
w_{c}
\tilde{w}_{c}
\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}
\mathbb{R}

Figure 8
:
Parts of the contours
\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}
and
\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}
from (
4.38
) and (
4.42
) close to where they cross the positive real axis in

w_{c}=1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2)
,
\tilde{w}_{c}=w_{c}+N^{-1/3}/2
. The curve segments
\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta},\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}
are shown in dotted lines.

By virtue of (
4.41
), (
4.37
) and (
4.35
), for
z\in\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}\setminus\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}=\{z\in\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}:|z-1|>\delta\}
we have
\Re(f_{1}(z))\leq-c_{0,1}
for a
c_{0,1}>0
and we can take
c_{0,0}=c_{0,1}
. We choose
\Sigma
to be the part of the contours where
z\notin\gamma^{\delta}_{-1,0,1,w}
and/or
w\notin\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}
. Then the integral (
4.30
) is on

\displaystyle\Sigma\cup(\gamma^{\delta}_{-1,0,1,w}\cup\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta})=\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}\cup\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}.

(4.43)

On
\Sigma
,
\exists\,c_{0,0}>0
such that
\Re(f_{1}(z))<-c_{0,0}
and/or
-\Re(f_{1}(w))<-c_{0,0}
, and also
\Re(f_{1}(z)),-\Re(f_{1}(w))<c_{0,0}/4
. Furthermore, we can bound
\left|\frac{z-\alpha}{w(w-\alpha)}\frac{zw-1}{(z-w)(z^{2}-1)}\right|<C(\delta)N^{1/3}
where
C(\delta)
is a constant depending on
\delta
. So overall we may bound

N^{1/3}e^{N^{2/3}f_{2}(1)(u_{b}-u_{a})}\left|\int_{\Sigma}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}w\frac{zw-1}{(z^{2}-1)(z-w)}\frac{z-\alpha}{w(w-\alpha)}\frac{e^{Nf_{1}(z)+N^{2/3}u_{a}f_{2}(z)-N^{1/3}f_{3}(z)\xi}}{e^{Nf_{1}(w)+N^{2/3}u_{b}f_{2}(w)-N^{1/3}f_{3}(w)\xi^{\prime}}}\right|\leq Ce^{-Nc_{0,0}/2}

(4.44)

for a
C>0
.

For the integral on
\gamma^{\delta}_{-1,0,1,w}\cup\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta},
we do the change of variable

z=1+Zc_{q}N^{-1/3}\quad w=1+Wc_{q}N^{-1/3}.

(4.45)

Use now Taylor and define
F(Z,W)
via

\displaystyle

\displaystyle e^{N(f_{1}(z)-f_{1}(w))}e^{N^{2/3}(u_{a}f_{2}(z)-u_{b}f_{2}(w))}e^{N^{1/3}(\xi^{\prime}f_{3}(w)-\xi f_{3}(z))}

(4.46)

\displaystyle=e^{N^{2/3}(u_{a}f_{2}(1)-u_{b}f_{2}(1))}e^{Z^{3}/3+Z^{2}u_{a}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2-\xi Zc_{q}-W^{3}/3-W^{2}u_{b}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2+Wc_{q}\xi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\times e^{\mathcal{O}(Z^{4}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(Z^{3}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(Z^{2}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{4}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{3}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{2}N^{-1/3})}

\displaystyle=F(Z,W)e^{N^{2/3}(u_{a}f_{2}(1)-u_{b}f_{2}(1))}

and denote
\tilde{F}(Z,W)=e^{Z^{3}/3+Z^{2}u_{a}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2-\xi Zc_{q}-W^{3}/3-W^{2}u_{b}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2+Wc_{q}\xi^{\prime}}
.
To control the contribution from the error terms in (
4.46
), we use the inequality
|e^{x}-1|\leq e^{|x|}|x|
.

With the change of variable (
4.45
), we have to control

A:=\Big|\frac{c_{q}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int_{(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}-1)\frac{N^{1/3}}{c_{q}}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{(\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}-1)\frac{N^{1/3}}{c_{q}}}\mathrm{d}W\frac{Z+2v}{W+2v}\frac{Z+W+ZWN^{-1/3}c_{q}}{(1+Wc_{q}N^{-1/3})(Z-W)Z(2+Zc_{q}N^{-1/3})}\\
\times(F(Z,W)-\tilde{F}(Z,W))\Big|.

(4.47)

We have

\begin{split}A\leq&\frac{c_{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}-1)\frac{N^{1/3}}{c_{q}}}\left|\mathrm{d}Z\right|\int_{(\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}-1)\frac{N^{1/3}}{c_{q}}}\left|\mathrm{d}W\right|\left|\frac{Z+2v}{W+2v}\frac{Z+W+ZWN^{-1/3}c_{q}}{(Z-W)Z}\tilde{F}(Z,W)\right|\\
&\times e^{|\mathcal{O}(Z^{4}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(Z^{3}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(Z^{2}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{4}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{3}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{2}N^{-1/3})|}\\
&\times|\mathcal{O}(Z^{4}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(Z^{3}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(Z^{2}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{4}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{3}N^{-1/3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{2}N^{-1/3})|.\end{split}

(4.48)

This implies

\begin{split}A\leq&\frac{c_{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}-1)\frac{N^{\frac{1}{3}}}{c_{q}}}\left|\mathrm{d}Z\right|\int_{(\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}-1)\frac{N^{\frac{1}{3}}}{c_{q}}}\left|\mathrm{d}W\right|\left|\frac{Z+2v}{W+2v}\frac{Z+W+ZWN^{-\frac{1}{3}}c_{q}}{(Z-W)Z}\frac{e^{Z^{3}(1+\chi_{1})/3-\xi Z(1+\chi_{2})c_{q}}}{e^{W^{3}(1+\chi_{3})/3-W(1+\chi_{4})c_{q}\xi^{\prime}}}\right|\\
&\times\left|\frac{e^{Z^{2}(1+\chi_{5})u_{a}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2}}{e^{W^{2}(1+\chi_{6})u_{b}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2}}\right|N^{-1/3}|\mathcal{O}(Z^{4})+\mathcal{O}(Z^{3})+\mathcal{O}(Z^{2})+\mathcal{O}(W^{4})+\mathcal{O}(W^{3})+\mathcal{O}(W^{2})|\end{split}

(4.49)

where the
\chi_{i}\in\mathbb{R},i=1,\ldots,6
can be taken as small in absolute value as desired by taking
\delta
small. Now for large
N
, the
|e^{Z^{3}(1+\chi_{1})/3}e^{-W^{3}(1+\chi_{3})/3}|
term dominates the integral. At the integration boundary, it is of order
e^{-\mathcal{O}(\delta^{3}N)}
. This easily implies that
N^{1/3}A
remains bounded as
N\to\infty
. Consequently,
A
vanishes like
N^{-1/3}
. We can thus take
\tilde{F}(Z,W)
instead of
F(Z,W)
and only make an error of
\mathcal{O}(N^{-1/3})
. By doing so, we are left with

\frac{c_{q}}{(2\pi\mathsf{i})^{2}}\int_{(\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}^{\delta}-1)\frac{N^{1/3}}{c_{q}}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{(\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}^{\delta}-1)\frac{N^{1/3}}{c_{q}}}\mathrm{d}W\frac{Z+2v}{W+2v}\frac{Z+W+ZWc_{q}N^{-1/3}}{(Z-W)Z}\frac{(2+Zc_{q}N^{-1/3})^{-1}}{1+WN^{-1/3}c_{q}}\tilde{F}(Z,W).

(4.50)

Finally, in (
4.50
) we can extend the curves to infinity (inside
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\Re(z^{3})<0\}
and
\{w\in\mathbb{C}:\Re(w^{3})>0\}
) and thus only make an error
e^{-\mathcal{O}(N)}.

We can then deform the contours to be as in (
4.5
) without errors.

To summarize, we have shown that

N^{1/3}e^{f_{2}(1)N^{2/3}(u_{b}-u_{a})}K_{1,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=\frac{c_{q}}{(2\pi\mathsf{i})^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{A_{Z}}^{\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{A_{W}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}W\frac{(Z+W+ZWc_{q}N^{-1/3})(Z+2v)}{(W+2v)Z(Z-W)}\\
\times\frac{e^{Z^{3}/3+Z^{2}u_{a}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2-\xi Zc_{q}-W^{3}/3-W^{2}u_{b}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2+Wc_{q}\xi^{\prime}}}{(1+WN^{-1/3}c_{q})(2+Zc_{q}N^{-1/3})}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1/3}+e^{-Nc_{0,0}/2}+e^{-\mathcal{O}(N)}),

(4.51)

implying

\begin{split}\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{1/3}e^{f_{2}(1)N^{2/3}(u_{b}-u_{a})}K_{1,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=&\frac{c_{q}}{(2\pi\mathsf{i})^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{A_{Z}}^{\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{A_{W}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}W\frac{(Z+W)(Z+2v)}{2(W+2v)Z(Z-W)}\\
&\times e^{Z^{3}/3+Z^{2}u_{a}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2-\xi Zc_{q}-W^{3}/3-W^{2}u_{b}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2+Wc_{q}\xi^{\prime}}\end{split}

(4.52)

for
A_{Z}>A_{W}>-2v,A_{Z}>0
.

If now
a>b,
we have the condition
|z|<|w|
on our contours. Deforming them so as to equal
(
4.42
), (
4.38
), we pick up an extra residue, which equals

N^{1/3}e^{f_{2}(1)N^{2/3}(u_{b}-u_{a})}\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{0}}\mathrm{d}ze^{N^{2/3}f_{2}(z)(u_{a}-u_{b})+N^{1/3}f_{3}(z)(\xi^{\prime}-\xi)}z^{-1}.

(4.53)

For
\gamma_{0}(s)=e^{\mathrm{i}s}
the contribution of the integral over
\gamma_{0}^{\delta}
in (
4.53
) clearly vanishes. Note
u_{a}-u_{b}<0
. On
\gamma_{0}^{\delta}
we do the same change of variable
z=1+Zc_{q}N^{-1/3}
. Next we Taylor-expand
f_{2}(1+Zc_{q}N^{-1/3}),f_{3}(1+Zc_{q}N^{-1/3})
as before, and control the contribution from the remainder terms as before. Sending then
N\to\infty
shows that (
4.53
) converges to

\frac{-c_{q}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}}\mathrm{d}Ze^{Z^{2}c_{q}^{2}f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(1)(u_{a}-u_{b})/2}e^{Zc_{q}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi)},

(4.54)

with
\mathrm{i}\mathbb{R}
oriented with increasing imaginary part.

Next we come to
K_{1,1}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})
. We have

\begin{split}K_{1,1}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=&\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathsf{i})^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{-1,0,1}}\mathrm{d}z\oint_{\gamma_{-1,0,1}}\mathrm{d}we^{N(f_{1}(z)+f_{1}(w))+N^{2/3}(u_{a}f_{2}(z)+u_{b}f_{2}(w))}\\
&\times\frac{e^{-N^{1/3}(f_{3}(z)\xi+f_{3}(w)\xi^{\prime})}(z-w)}{(z^{2}-1)(w^{2}-1)(zw-1)}(z-\alpha)(w-\alpha).\end{split}

(4.55)

We can choose the contour (
4.42
) for both
z
and
w
in (
4.55
). Redoing all the steps made for
K_{1,2}
we obtain

\begin{split}\lim_{N\to\infty}N^{2/3}e^{-N^{2/3}f_{2}(1)(u_{a}+u_{b})}K_{1,1}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})=&\frac{c_{q}^{2}}{(2\pi\mathsf{i})^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{\pi/3}_{1}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{\pi/3}_{1}}\mathrm{d}W\frac{(Z-W)(W+2v)(Z+2v)}{4ZW(Z+W)}\\
&\times e^{Z^{3}/3+Z^{2}u_{a}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2-\xi Zc_{q}+W^{3}/3+W^{2}u_{b}c_{q}^{2}f^{\prime\prime}_{2}(1)/2-Wc_{q}\xi^{\prime}}.\end{split}

(4.56)

Finally, we come to
K_{2,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})
. We have

\begin{split}K_{2,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})&=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}}\mathrm{d}z\oint_{\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha,1/z}}\mathrm{d}we^{-N(f_{1}(z)+f_{1}(w))-N^{2/3}(u_{a}f_{2}(z)+u_{b}f_{2}(w))+N^{1/3}(f_{3}(z)\xi+f_{3}(w)\xi^{\prime})}\\
&\times\frac{z-w}{zw(z-\alpha)(w-\alpha)(zw-1)}.\end{split}

(4.57)

We can choose the contour for
z
to also contain
1/\alpha
, e.g.
\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}(s)=(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2))e^{\mathrm{i}s}.
Note that on the unit circle,
\Re(-f_{2}(z)),\Re(-f_{2}(1/z))
are maximal at
z=1
and decrease until they reach
z=-1
.

We consider first the case
u_{a}+u_{b}>0
. We start with the residue of
w
at
1/z
. It equals

\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2))e^{\mathrm{i}s}}\mathrm{d}z\frac{z^{2}-1}{z(z-\alpha)(-\alpha)(z-1/\alpha)}e^{-N^{2/3}(f_{2}(z)u_{a}+f_{2}(1/z)u_{b})}e^{N^{1/3}(f_{3}(z)\xi-f_{3}(z)\xi^{\prime})}.

(4.58)

Repeating the steps of the asymptotics for
K_{1,2},
one then obtains

\lim_{N\to\infty}e^{N^{2/3}f_{2}(1)(u_{a}+u_{b})}\eqref{res1/z}=\frac{-1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{2\pi/3}_{B_{1}}}\mathrm{d}Z\frac{2Z}{(Z+2v)(Z-2v)}e^{-Z^{2}f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(1)c_{q}^{2}(u_{a}+u_{b})/2+Zc_{q}(\xi-\xi^{\prime})}

(4.59)

with
B_{1}>2|v|
.

Next we consider the contribution from the pole at
w=\alpha
, given by

\frac{e^{N^{2/3}f_{2}(1)(u_{a}+u_{b})}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2))e^{\mathrm{i}s}}\mathrm{d}z\frac{e^{-N(f_{1}(z)+f_{1}(\alpha))-N^{2/3}(f_{2}(z)u_{a}+f_{2}(\alpha)u_{b})+N^{1/3}(f_{3}(z)\xi+f_{3}(\alpha)\xi^{\prime})}}{z\alpha(z\alpha-1)}

(4.60)

We may deform the contour
(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2))e^{\mathrm{i}s}
in (
4.60
) to

(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2))(1+\varepsilon_{1}L(e^{\mathrm{i}s}))e^{\mathrm{i}s},s\in[0,2\pi].

(4.61)

without errors. By the same asymptotic analysis performed for
K_{1,2}
we then see that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\eqref{polewa}=\frac{e^{8v^{3}/3-2v^{2}c_{q}^{2}f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(1)u_{b}-2vc_{q}\xi^{\prime}}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{B_{2}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Z\frac{1}{Z-2v}e^{-Z^{3}/3-Z^{2}u_{a}c_{q}^{2}f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(1)/2+\xi Zc_{q}},\qquad B_{2}>2v.

(4.62)

We compute the remaining term

\begin{split}\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{e^{N^{2/3}f_{2}(1)(u_{a}+u_{b})}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint_{\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}}\mathrm{d}z\oint_{\gamma_{\sqrt{q}}}\mathrm{d}w&e^{-N(f_{1}(z)+f_{1}(w))-N^{2/3}(u_{a}f_{2}(z)+u_{b}f_{2}(w))+N^{1/3}(f_{3}(z)\xi+f_{3}(w)\xi^{\prime})}\\
&\times\frac{z-w}{zw(z-\alpha)(w-\alpha)(zw-1)}.\end{split}

(4.63)

As contours, we can choose (
4.61
) for
\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}
, and
\frac{1-N^{-1/3}(4|v|c_{q}+1)}{1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2)}\times\eqref{gammaqal}
for
\gamma_{\sqrt{q}}
(note that with this choice,
\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}
has no point of
\gamma_{\sqrt{q}}
in its interior, and vice versa).

With this choice, we can now proceed exactly as before. This then yields that

\displaystyle\eqref{limq}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{2\pi/3}_{B_{3}}}\mathrm{d}Z\int_{\mathfrak{G}^{2\pi/3}_{B_{4}}}\mathrm{d}W

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-Z^{3}/3-u_{a}Z^{2}c_{q}^{2}f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(1)/2+Zc_{q}\xi}}{e^{W^{3}/3+u_{b}W^{2}c_{q}^{2}f_{2}^{\prime\prime}(1)/2-Wc_{q}\xi^{\prime}}}\frac{Z-W}{(W+2v)(Z+2v)(Z+W)}

(4.64)

for
B_{3}>-2v,B_{4}<-2v
and
B_{3}<-B_{4}
.

For
u_{a}=u_{b}=0,
we proceed as follows. Writing the integer parts, the residue of
w=1/z
is given by

\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{(1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2))e^{\mathrm{i}s}}\mathrm{d}z\frac{z^{2}-1}{(-\alpha)(z-1/\alpha)(z-\alpha)}z^{\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi\rfloor-\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}\rfloor-1}.

(4.65)

Now, if
\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi\rfloor-\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}\rfloor\leq-1,
we can send
z\to\infty
in (
4.65
) which shows
\eqref{res(z,1/z)}=0.
If
\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi\rfloor-\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}\rfloor>0
, (
4.65
) equals the sum of the residues at
z=\alpha,1/\alpha.
and if
\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi\rfloor-\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}\rfloor=0,
(
4.65
) equals the sum of the residues at
z=0,\alpha,1/\alpha
. Thus

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\eqref{res(z,1/z)}

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}\big(-\mathbf{1}_{\{\frac{\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi\rfloor-\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}\rfloor}{N^{1/3}}>0\}}\left(e^{-2vc_{q}\frac{\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi\rfloor-\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}\rfloor}{N^{1/3}}}+e^{2vc_{q}\frac{\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi\rfloor-\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}\rfloor}{N^{1/3}}}\right)

(4.66)

\displaystyle-\mathbf{1}_{\{\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi\rfloor-\lfloor N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}\rfloor=0\}}\alpha^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{1}_{\{\xi-\xi^{\prime}>0\}}(e^{-2vc_{q}(\xi-\xi^{\prime})}+e^{2vc_{q}(\xi-\xi^{\prime})})-\mathbf{1}_{\{\xi=\xi^{\prime}\}}.

The contribution (
4.60
) from the pole at
w=\alpha
is as before, but here we compute separately the residue
z=1/\alpha
in (
4.60
), which equals

\frac{1}{\alpha}e^{f_{3}(\alpha)N^{1/3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi)}\to_{N\to\infty}e^{2vc_{q}(\xi-\xi^{\prime})}.

(4.67)

What thus remains to compute from (
4.60
) is the limit of

\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\oint_{\gamma_{\sqrt{q}}}\mathrm{d}z\frac{1}{z\alpha(z\alpha-1)}e^{-N(f(\alpha)+f(z))}e^{N^{1/3}\xi^{\prime}f_{3}(\alpha)}e^{N^{1/3}\xi f_{3}(z)},

(4.68)

which equals

\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathfrak{G}_{C_{1}}^{2\pi/3}}\mathrm{d}Ze^{-Z^{3}/3+Z\xi c_{q}}\frac{e^{8v^{3}/3-2vc_{q}\xi^{\prime}}}{Z-2v},\qquad C_{1}<2v.

(4.69)

So in total, the sum of the residues of
w
in
1/z
and
\alpha
converges to

\eqref{BBB}+\mathrm{sgn}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi)e^{-2vc_{q}|\xi-\xi^{\prime}|}.

(4.70)

The remaining term is exactly
(
4.64
)
for
u_{a}=u_{b}=0
.
∎

The following proposition will provide the required integrable upper bound.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
c_{q},K,x_{a},x_{b},\alpha
be as in Proposition
4.5
. Let
L>0
and
\xi,\xi^{\prime}>-L
and
a\leq b
. Then there is an
N_{0}
and a
C>0
(which may depend on
L,u_{a},u_{b}
, but not on
\xi,\xi^{\prime}
) such that for
N>N_{0}

\displaystyle N^{1/3}e^{N^{2/3}\ln(1-\sqrt{q})(u_{b}-u_{a})}|K_{1,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})|\leq Ce^{-c\xi+d\xi^{\prime}},

(4.71)

\displaystyle N^{2/3}e^{-N^{2/3}\ln(1-\sqrt{q})(u_{b}+u_{a})}|K_{1,1}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})|\leq Ce^{-c\xi-c\xi^{\prime}},

(4.72)

\displaystyle e^{N^{2/3}\ln(1-\sqrt{q})(u_{b}+u_{a})}|K_{2,2}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})|\leq Ce^{d\xi+d\xi^{\prime}},

(4.73)

with
c=2|v|c_{q}+3/4,d=2|v|c_{q}+2/3.

Proof.

We assume first that
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\geq 0
. To show (
4.72
), note that the proof of pointwise convergence easily implies that for
\xi=\xi^{\prime}=0
and the contour (
4.42
) for
\gamma_{-1,0,1}
one has

\begin{split}N^{2/3}\frac{|K_{1,1}(a,x_{a};b,x_{b})|}{e^{N^{2/3}f_{2}(1)(u_{a}+u_{b})}}\leq\frac{N^{2/3}}{e^{N^{2/3}f_{2}(1)(u_{a}+u_{b})}}\oint_{\gamma_{-1,0,1}}|\mathrm{d}z|\oint_{\gamma_{-1,0,1}}&|\mathrm{d}w|\big|e^{N(f_{1}(z)+f_{1}(w))+N^{2/3}(u_{a}f_{2}(z)+u_{b}f_{2}(w))}\big|\\
&\times\left|\frac{(z-\alpha)(w-\alpha)(z-w)}{(z^{2}-1)(w^{2}-1)(zw-1)}\right|\leq C\end{split}

(4.74)

where
f_{1},f_{2}
are as in the proof of Proposition
4.5
. If now
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\geq 0
, this creates an extra factor

|e^{-N^{1/3}(\ln(z)\xi+\ln(w)\xi^{\prime})}|.

(4.75)

For the contour (
4.42
), we have that for
z,w\in\gamma_{-1,0,1}

|z|,|w|\geq 1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1)
. Hence we can bound for
N
large enough
|e^{-N^{1/3}(\ln(z)\xi+\ln(w)\xi^{\prime})}|\leq e^{-c(\xi+\xi^{\prime})}.

Next we come to (
4.71
). As contours we choose again
\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}=\eqref{gammaq},\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}=\eqref{gamma-101}.
The proof of pointwise convergence easily implies that for
\xi=\xi^{\prime}=0
we have a constant upper bound.
If now
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\geq 0,
then we get an extra factor which equals

|e^{N^{1/3}\ln(w)\xi^{\prime}-N^{1/3}\ln(z)\xi}|.

(4.76)

Now for
w\in\gamma_{\sqrt{q},\alpha}
, we have
|w|\leq 1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1/2)
. Consequently, for
N
large enough, we have
N^{1/3}\ln(|w|)\leq 2|v|c_{q}+2/3
. Since for
z\in\gamma_{-1,0,1,w}
we have
|z|\geq 1+N^{-1/3}(2|v|c_{q}+1)
we get
-N^{1/3}\ln(|z|)\leq-(2|v|c_{q}+3/4)
. This implies that
\eqref{factor1}\leq e^{-c\xi+d\xi^{\prime}}
. As for
K_{2,2}
, consider the case
u_{a}+u_{b}>0
first. In (
4.60
), by definition of
\alpha
and choice of contour, we obtain
|\eqref{polewa}|\leq Ce^{d(\xi+\xi^{\prime})}
. Equally, one obtains
|\eqref{res1/z}|e^{N^{2/3}\ln(1-\sqrt{q})(u_{b}+u_{a})}\leq e^{d(\xi+\xi^{\prime})}
. Finally, (
4.63
) can be bounded by
e^{d(\xi+\xi^{\prime})}
as well, simply by the choice of contours. The case
u_{a}+u_{b}=0
is treated similarly.
Finally, if
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in[-L,0],
the proof(s) of pointwise convergence give an upper bound
C
, where the constant
C
may depend on
L
. If e.g.
\xi\in[-L,0],\xi^{\prime}>0
we multiply the bound obtained in the case
\xi\leq 0,\xi^{\prime}=0
by the bound obtained for
\xi^{\prime}\geq 0,\xi=0,
finishing the proof.
∎

5
Symmetric plane partitions

A
free boundary plane partition of length
N
is an array
(\pi_{i,j})_{1\leq j\leq i\leq N}
of nonnegative integers satisfying the properties
\pi_{i,j}\geq\pi_{i+1,j},\pi_{i,j}\geq\pi_{i,j+1}

for all meaningful
i,j
. Its
volume
is the sum of its entries:
|\pi|=\sum_{1\leq j\leq i\leq N}\pi_{i,j}.

Clearly a free boundary plane partition of length
N
is half of a
symmetric plane partition
with base in the square
N\times N
, which by definition is an array
(\pi_{i,j})_{1\leq i,j\leq N}
satisfying the above constraints plus the symmetry constraint
\pi_{i,j}=\pi_{j,i}
. An example of length 5 and volume 79 is depicted in Figure
9
.

6
7
3
9
5
2
9
