Appendix A
Fredholm pfaffians and point processes

For an antisymmetric
2n\times 2n
matrix
A,
the
pfaffian
of

A
is given by

\pf A=\frac{1}{2^{n}n!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)A_{\sigma(1),\sigma(2)}A_{\sigma(3),\sigma(4)}\cdots A_{\sigma(2n-1),\sigma(2n)}

(A.1)

and one can show that
(\pf A)^{2}=\det A
.

Matrix kernels and Fredholm pfaffians.

Here we briefly introduce the notions needed for the text. For more
information, see
[
Rai00
]
or
[
OQR17
, Appendix B]
.
A
matrix kernel
on a space
X
is a matrix-valued function on
X\times X
. It is said
antisymmetric
if
K(x,y)=-K(y,x)^{T}
. We use
[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
to denote the block matrix whose block
(i,j)
is
K(x_{i},x_{j})
. If the kernel
K
is antisymmetric and of even size, then so is the matrix
[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}
and we can compute its pfaffian. Note that this pfaffian is invariant under a permutation of
S=\{x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n}\}
and we can use
\pf K(S)
to denote it.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
K
,
\tilde{K}
be
2\times 2
antisymmetric kernels on
X
such that there exists a complex-valued function
f
on
X
such that

\tilde{K}(x,y):=\begin{pmatrix}[l]e^{f(x)}&0\\
0&e^{-f(x)}\end{pmatrix}K(x,y)\begin{pmatrix}[l]e^{f(y)}&0\\
0&e^{-f(y)}\end{pmatrix}.

(A.2)

Then

\pf[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}=\pf[\tilde{K}(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}.

(A.3)

Let
(X,{m})
be a measure space and
K
a scalar kernel. The
Fredholm determinant
is defined as

\det(I+K)_{X}:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{X^{n}}\mathrm{d}m^{n}(x)\det[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}

(A.4)

where
I(x,y)=\delta_{x,y}
. For a
2\times 2
antisymmetric matrix kernel
K
the
Fredholm pfaffian
is defined in analogous way by

\pf(J+K)_{X}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\int_{X^{n}}\mathrm{d}{m}^{n}(x)\pf[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}

(A.5)

where
J
is the antisymmetric matrix kernel
J(x,y)=\delta_{x,y}\begin{pmatrix}[c]0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}.

When
X
is finite and
m
is a counting measure then
\pf(J+K)_{X}
coincides with the ordinary pfaffian of
J+K
. Identities involving ordinary pfaffians and determinants can usually be generalized to the Fredholm setting, e.g.
\pf\left(J+K\right)_{X}^{2}=\det(I-JK)_{X}
where
I=\delta_{x,y}\begin{pmatrix}[c]1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}.

The Fredholm determinant (
A.4
) is absolutely convergent if we assume that a scalar kernel
K
satisfies
|K(x,y)|\leq D
for all
(x,y)\in X\times X
and
\int_{X}\mathrm{d}m(x)=M<\infty
since by Hadamard’s bound

\lvert\det[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\rvert\leq n^{n/2}D^{n},
which gives us that
(
A.4
) is dominated by the absolutely convergent series

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{n^{n/2}(DM)^{n}}{n!}.

(A.6)

A little bit more generally, to check that the Fredholm pfaffian is dominated by the series (
A.6
) it is enough to have that the antisymmetric kernel
K
satisfies

\lvert\pf[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\rvert\leq n^{n/2}{D}^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{n}f(x_{i}),

(A.7)

for some measurable function
f
such that
\int_{X}\mathrm{d}m(x)f(x)=M<\infty
.

Pfaffian point processes.

We now briefly introduce point processes. For more detail see for example
[
Joh06
]
or
[
BO17
]
. Let
{X}
be a locally compact Polish space. A configuration is any subset of
{X}
with no accumulation points. Let
\mathit{Conf}({X})
be the set of all configurations and let
\mathbb{P}
be a probability measure on
\mathit{Conf}({X})
.
\mathbb{P}
induces a Radon measure on
X^{n}
, assigning to a bounded Borel set
S
the expected number of
n
-tuples of distinct points that fall in
S
. If this measure is absolutely continuous, then it has the Radon–Nikodym derivative
\rho_{n}
, known as the
n
-point correlation function, with respect to some reference measure
m
. For a bounded Borel set
B\subseteq X
, the gap probability
p_{B}
, which is the probability that a random configuration has no intersection with
B
, is given by

p_{B}=\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i}(1-\chi_{B}(x_{i}))\right]=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\int_{B^{n}}\mathrm{d}m^{n}(x)\rho_{n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n}).

(A.8)

In the case of a discrete set
{X}
endowed with the counting measure, the correlation function has a simple interpretation. For an
n
-tuple of distinct points
S=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}
, the
n
-point correlation function
\rho_{n}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})

is equal to
\mathbb{P}(C_{S})
where
C_{S}
is the set of all configurations that contain
S
. For
B
finite, the gap probability formula says

p_{B}=\sum_{S\subseteq B}(-1)^{\#S}\mathbb{P}(C_{S}),

(A.9)

which is simply the inclusion–exclusion formula.

A point process is called
pfaffian
if its
n
-point correlation function can be written in terms of a
2\times 2
antisymmetric matrix kernel
K
, called the correlation kernel, as

\rho_{n}(x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n})=\pf[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}.

(A.10)

For a pfaffian point process, the gap probability for a bounded Borel set
B\subseteq X
is given by the Fredholm pfaffian

p_{B}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\int_{B^{n}}\mathrm{d}{m}^{n}(x)\pf[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}=\pf(J-K)_{B}.

(A.11)

Remark A.2
.

In Section
4
we investigate the gap probabilities of
B=(s_{1},\infty)\times\cdots\times(s_{k},\infty)\subset(\mathbb{Z}^{\prime})^{k}
where
m
is the counting measure.
B
is not bounded in this case, but since it can be written as the union of bounded sets, (
A.11
) holds by the dominated convergence theorem as long as we can claim the Fredholm pfaffian is absolutely convergent. To show it is absolutely convergent we use the lemma below which gives a sufficient condition for (
A.7
) to be satisfied. In this case we will use
f(x)=e^{-bx}
,
b>0
for which
\int_{B}\mathrm{d}m(x)e^{-bx}
is indeed finite.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
K=\begin{pmatrix}[l]K_{1,1}&K_{1,2}\\
K_{2,1}&K_{2,2}\end{pmatrix}
be a
2\times 2
antisymmetric kernel and
c>d\geq 0
. Let
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
and suppose there is a
C>0
such that

|K_{1,1}(x,y)|\leq Ce^{-cx-cy},\quad|K_{1,2}(x,y)|\leq Ce^{-cx+dy},\quad|K_{2,2}(x,y)|\leq Ce^{dx+dy}.

(A.12)

Then there exist
b>0
and
D>0
such that

\lvert\pf[K(x_{i},x_{j})]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\rvert\leq n^{n/2}D^{n}e^{-b\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}}.

(A.13)

Proof.

Define

\tilde{K}(x,x^{\prime})=\begin{pmatrix}[l]e^{cx}&0\\
0&e^{-dx}\end{pmatrix}K(x,y)\begin{pmatrix}[l]e^{cy}&0\\
0&e^{-dy}\end{pmatrix}.

(A.14)

Let
S=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}.
Then
\lvert\pf K(S)\rvert=e^{(d-c)\sum_{i=1}^{n}{x_{i}}}\lvert\pf\tilde{K}(S)|\leq e^{(d-c)\sum_{i=1}^{n}{x_{i}}}\sqrt{(2n)^{n}C^{2n}}
where the inequality comes from Hadamard’s bound (since
\tilde{K}(x_{i},x_{j})<C
). We choose
D=C\sqrt{2}
and
b=c-d
.
∎

Appendix B
Pochhammer, theta and some elementary asymptotics

For
q
a parameter, the
q
-Pochhammer symbol of length
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the multiplicative, and the additive, theta functions are defined by:

(x;q)_{n}:=\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(1-xq^{i}),\qquad\theta_{q}(x):=(x;q)_{\infty}(q/x;q)_{\infty},\qquad\theta_{3}(z;q):=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}q^{\frac{n^{2}}{2}}z^{n}

(B.1)

where
|q|<1
. For finite
n
we have
(x;q)_{n}=(x;q)_{\infty}/(q^{n}x;q)_{\infty}
1
1

1

This should be taken as the definition for negative integer values of
n
.
. They are related by the Jacobi triple product identity
[
Dol98
]
:

\theta_{3}(z;q)=(q;q)_{\infty}\prod_{i=\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2},\frac{5}{2},\dots}\left(1+q^{i}z\right)\left(1+q^{i}/z\right)=(q;q)_{\infty}\theta_{q}(-z\sqrt{q}).

(B.2)

Finally note the quasi-periodicity relations:

\theta_{q}(qx)=-\frac{1}{x}\theta_{q}(x),\qquad\theta_{q}\left(\frac{1}{x}\right)=-\frac{1}{x}\theta_{q}(x),\qquad\theta_{q}\left(\frac{x}{q}\right)=-\frac{x}{q}\theta_{q}(x).

(B.3)

We use the convention that concatenation means product:
(a_{1},a_{2},\dots;q)_{n}:=\prod_{i}(a_{i};q)_{n}
and similarly
\theta_{q}(a_{1},a_{2},\dots):=\prod_{i}\theta_{q}(a_{i}).

We now turn to certain limits of Pochhammer symbols (and hence of theta functions). First we need the dilogarithm function
\mathrm{Li}_{2}(z)
. It is defined by the power series representation

\mathrm{Li}_{2}(z)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{z^{2}}{n^{2}},\qquad|z|<1

(B.4)

with analytic continuation given by

\mathrm{Li}_{2}(z)=-\int_{0}^{z}\frac{\log(1-u)}{u}\mathrm{d}u,\quad z\in\mathbb{C}\backslash[1,\infty).

(B.5)

Differentiating the dilogarithm, we obtain the usual logarithm
\left(z\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}z}\right)\mathrm{Li}_{2}(z)=-\log(1-z).
If
q=\exp(-r)
and
r\to 0+
, we have

\log(z;q)_{\infty}\sim-\frac{\mathrm{Li}_{2}(z)}{r}

(B.6)

while

\log(z;q)_{n}\sim\begin{cases}\frac{1}{r}(\mathrm{Li}_{2}(e^{-A}z)-\mathrm{Li}_{2}(z))&\text{if $nr\to A>0$,}\\
n\log(1-z)&\text{if $nr\to 0$.}\end{cases}

(B.7)

Using this, one can prove that if
u(q)
is a function that tends to
u\in(0,1)
as
r\to 0+
, then for fixed
a
and
b

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}q=e^{-r},\\
r\to 0+\end{subarray}}\frac{(q^{a}u(q);q)_{\infty}}{(q^{b}u(q);q)_{\infty}}=(1-u)^{b-a}.

(B.8)

Appendix C
Fermionic propagators

Proposition C.1
.

Using the notation of (
3.18
) and (
3.67
), we have:

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}\sqrt{zw}(z-w)}{(z+v)(w+v)(zw-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(zw-v^{2})}{(z-w)(w-v)(z+v)},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,\left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(zw-v^{2})}{(z-w)(w+v)(z-v)},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,\left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}\sqrt{zw}(z-w)}{(z-v)(w-v)(zw-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v^{er}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}(z-w)}{\sqrt{zw}(zw-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v^{2}}{zw}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v^{er}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{w\sqrt{w}(zw-v^{2})}{\sqrt{z}(z-w)(w^{2}-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v^{er}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{z\sqrt{z}(zw-v^{2})}{\sqrt{w}(z-w)(z^{2}-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v^{er}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}zw\sqrt{zw}(z-w)}{(z^{2}-v^{2})(w^{2}-v^{2})(zw-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v^{ec}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}zw\sqrt{zw}(z-w)}{(z^{2}-v^{2})(w^{2}-v^{2})(zw-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v^{ec}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{z\sqrt{z}(zw-v^{2})}{\sqrt{w}(z-w)(z^{2}-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,

(C.1)

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v^{ec}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{w\sqrt{w}(zw-v^{2})}{\sqrt{z}(z-w)(w^{2}-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v^{ec}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}(z-w)}{\sqrt{zw}(zw-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v^{2}}{zw}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v^{\beta,\ or}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}\sqrt{zw}(z-w)}{(zw-v^{2})(z+\beta)(w+\beta)},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v^{2}}{zw}\right|<1,\left|\frac{\beta}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{\beta}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v^{\beta,\ or}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(zw-v^{2})(w+\beta)}{(z-w)(w^{2}-v^{2})(z+\beta)},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,\left|\frac{\beta}{z}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v^{\beta,\ or}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(zw-v^{2})(z+\beta)}{(z-w)(z^{2}-v^{2})(w+\beta)},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{\beta}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v^{\beta,\ or}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}\sqrt{zw}(z-w)(z+\beta)(w+\beta)}{(z^{2}-v^{2})(w^{2}-v^{2})(zw-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}\sqrt{zw}(z-w)(z-\alpha)(w-\alpha)}{(z^{2}-v^{2})(w^{2}-v^{2})(zw-v^{2})},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(zw-v^{2})(z-\alpha)}{(z-w)(z^{2}-v^{2})(w-\alpha)},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{\alpha}{w}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{zw}(zw-v^{2})(w-\alpha)}{(z-w)(w^{2}-v^{2})(z-\alpha)},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,\left|\frac{\alpha}{z}\right|<1,

\displaystyle\langle 0|\psi^{*}(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v^{\alpha,\ oc}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{v^{2}\sqrt{zw}(z-w)}{(zw-v^{2})(z-\alpha)(w-\alpha)},\ \text{for}\ \left|\frac{v^{2}}{zw}\right|<1,\left|\frac{\alpha}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{\alpha}{w}\right|<1.

Proof.

Ping-pong (one-sided) and the boson–fermion correspondence (
3.17
). Equivalently, the argument proving (
3.64
) applies throughout.
∎

We now turn to the (unmodified) two free boundary propagators. Recall definition (
3.40
).

Proposition C.2
.

We have:

\displaystyle\langle\underline{u,t}|\psi(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v,t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{((uv)^{2};(uv)^{2})^{2}_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(\frac{w}{z})}{(-\frac{v}{z},-\frac{v}{w},uz,uw;uv)_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(u^{2}zw)}\cdot\frac{\theta_{3}\left((\frac{tzw}{v^{2}})^{2};(uv)^{4}\right)}{(uv;uv)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{v^{2}}{tw\sqrt{zw}},

\displaystyle for\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,|uz|<1,|uw|<1,|uv|<1,

\displaystyle\langle\underline{u,t}|\psi(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v,t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{((uv)^{2};(uv)^{2})^{2}_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(u^{2}zw)}{(-\frac{v}{z},\frac{v}{w},uz,-uw;uv)_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(\frac{w}{z})}\cdot\frac{\theta_{3}\left((\frac{tz}{w})^{2};(uv)^{4}\right)}{(uv;uv)_{\infty}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{w}{z}},

\displaystyle for\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,|uz|<1,|uw|<1,\left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,|uv|<1,

(C.2)

\displaystyle\langle\underline{u,t}|\psi^{*}(z)\psi(w)|\underline{v,t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{((uv)^{2};(uv)^{2})^{2}_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(u^{2}zw)}{(\frac{v}{z},-\frac{v}{w},-uz,uw;uv)_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(\frac{w}{z})}\cdot\frac{\theta_{3}\left((\frac{tw}{z})^{2};(uv)^{4}\right)}{(uv;uv)_{\infty}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{z}{w}},

\displaystyle for\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,|uz|<1,|uw|<1,\left|\frac{w}{z}\right|<1,|uv|<1,

\displaystyle\langle\underline{u,t}|\psi^{*}(z)\psi^{*}(w)|\underline{v,t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{((uv)^{2};(uv)^{2})^{2}_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(\frac{w}{z})}{(\frac{v}{z},\frac{v}{w},-uz,-uw;uv)_{\infty}\theta_{(uv)^{2}}(u^{2}zw)}\cdot\frac{\theta_{3}\left((\frac{tv^{2}}{zw})^{2};(uv)^{4}\right)}{(uv;uv)_{\infty}}\cdot\frac{v^{2}t}{w\sqrt{zw}},

\displaystyle for\left|\frac{v}{z}\right|<1,\left|\frac{v}{w}\right|<1,|uz|<1,|uw|<1,|uv|<1.

Proof.

Ping-pong and the boson–fermion correspondence (
3.17
). All are similar to the proof of (
3.66
).
∎
